\subsection{N14 mi-skill-discovery}\label{node:S3-N14-mi-skill-discovery}
\textbf{Relation} $-$: Opposite sign, same geometry. Skill as action makes I(S;w) our middle term at a skill prior; DADS maximises I(S';w\textbar{}S) but minimises H(S'\textbar{}S,w), our sign on noise. Eysenbach 2021: a convex function on the state-marginal polytope, vertex skills, and H(\ensuremath{\bar{\rho}}) = I(S;w) + E\_w H(\ensuremath{\rho}\textbar{}w), the Jensen gap our concavity avoids paying. DIAYN also maximises H(A\textbar{}S). Two papers run our loop with the maximised sign: EDL, Campos 2020, fixes p(s) as a reference and trains each skill on the frozen reward log q\_\ensuremath{\phi}(s\textbar{}z) (Eq. 9), our MM inner step per skill, and policy-induced estimates pay log N for known states and 0 for new ones, so the policy locks in; Zheng 2024 Eqs. 2 to 3 alternate the same way, and its Prop. 3 shows the METRA actor lower-bounds I(S,S';Z) minus a representation entropy that it minimises, our sign on another quantity. CIC, Laskin 2022, rewards the particle entropy H(\ensuremath{\tau}) alone (Eq. 6), so it is functionally a marginal-entropy maximiser. Modirshanechi 2026 reads goal-reaching as maximising I(goal; trajectory) and proves three goal-conditioned formulations have inequivalent optima; with a fixed goal this sits beside our small H(Z).

\textbf{Idea.} Maximise I(S;w), I(S';w\textbar{}S) or I(\ensuremath{\tau};w) between a latent skill and visited states.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Eysenbach-information21}: MISL maximises a convex function on the state-marginal polytope, vertex skills, and the Jensen gap H(rho)=I(S;w)+E H(rho\textbar{}w): geometric twin, opposite sign
\end{itemize}

\medskip\textsc{Group E. The state-to-action channel I(S;A): not a term of H(Z)}

\subsection{N17 information-cost-of-control}\label{node:S3-N17-information-cost-of-control}
\textbf{Relation} $\sim$: Different quantity, same "simple regulator" intent, opposite geometry at a fixed state law: there every such cost is convex in \ensuremath{\pi}, so optima are stochastic at finite \ensuremath{\beta}, while ours is concave and keeps a vertex. Sequentially this fails: Tanaka 2017's transfer-entropy-regularised MDP is nonconvex (Sec. IV-C), its value V(\ensuremath{\mu}) = min\_q \{E c + I(X;U)\} is concave in the state law and so pulls toward concentrated state laws, our direction, and forward-backward Blahut--Arimoto reaches only stationary points (Thm. 2). The cost pushes toward state-independent action, the opposite of "the regulator is a model", only while it binds: Sims 2003's static min E(Y\ensuremath{-}X)\textsuperscript{2} s.t. I(X;Y) \textless{} C is an error-controlled min H(Z) with a capacity cap, h(Z) = h(X) \ensuremath{-} C at the Gaussian optimum, requisite variety at equality, and deterministic once H(X) \ensuremath{\leq} C. Still--Precup maximise I(S,A;S') = I(S;Z) + I(A;Z\textbar{}S) while penalising I(S;A), the clearest anti-regulator. Tishby--Polani's uniform-prior environmental term raises transition noise, opposite to our third term. Arumugam--Van Roy 2023 penalise I(E;A) from environment or posterior sample to action, an epistemic-channel variant with BayesRegret \ensuremath{\leq} \ensuremath{\surd}(\textbar{}A\textbar{} T R(D)/2) + TD (Thm. 1), relevant to our Thompson-style 2b. Gottwald--Braun 2019 build decision costs from inverse Pigou--Dalton transfers, so any majorisation-monotone cost qualifies.

\textbf{Idea.} Penalise I(S;A) or KL(\ensuremath{\pi}\ensuremath{\|}prior): information-to-go, thermodynamic bounded rationality, rational inattention, policy compression, transfer-entropy costs, and more generally any cost monotone in the majorisation preorder.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Still-information12}: Maximises I(S,A;S')=I(S;Z)+I(A;Z\textbar{}S) under the occupancy while penalising I(S;A): the clearest anti-regulator
\end{itemize}

\subsection{N18 relevant-information}\label{node:S3-N18-relevant-information}
\textit{Merged from} N19 plasticity-maximisation (2). \textbf{Relation} $\approx$: Same mechanism, different selection rule. Polani 2001, F correction, uses a maximum-entropy tie-break over optimal actions, the barycentre of the optimal face; the minimum-I(S;A) version is only future work there and appears in Polani 2006 and van Dijk--Polani. On the three-state example with optimal sets \{a1,a2\}, \{a2,a3\}, \{a3,a1\} both give the 0.585-bit stochastic policy against 0.918 bits for the best deterministic one. Kolchinsky--Wolpert: viability = \ensuremath{-}H(own state), the only paper here with our sign. Tishby 1999 gives the Gibbs encoder and the alternating Blahut--Arimoto iteration (Thms. 4, 5); Abel 2019 minimises H(S\_\ensuremath{\phi}) + \ensuremath{\beta} E KL(\ensuremath{\pi}\_E\ensuremath{\|}\ensuremath{\pi}\_\ensuremath{\phi}) and finds hard assignments beat soft ones. [N19 plasticity-maximisation] Same mechanism, different objective; opposite sign to N17 on the state-to-action channel. At the optimum H(A\textbar{}S, history) = 0 and H(A\textbar{}history) is maximal: the action is a deterministic, maximally varied function of state, the good-regulator model condition. Lucas 2026 Thm. A.2 restricts the max to deterministic policies by Bauer's maximum principle, the convex-maximisation mirror of our concave-minimisation vertex argument. Its proof treats the action marginal q as independent of \ensuremath{\pi}, although q is the marginal of \ensuremath{\pi}; the conclusion still holds because mutual information is convex in the channel for a fixed input law, and with q frozen the step is our freeze-and-solve. In the Control-Gated MDP the optimum carries about 0.92 bits of plasticity and 0 bits of empowerment. Abel 2025 pairs plasticity with empowerment and bounds their sum by generalised directed information: the good regulator sits at maximal plasticity and zero realised empowerment.

\textbf{Idea.} The state information needed to act optimally, traced across horizons, goals and persistence versus progeny; semantic information as viability-relevant information; bottleneck state abstraction.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed N19 plasticity-maximisation [\ensuremath{\approx}]: Maximise plasticity, the directed information from observations into actions, with per-step reward I(S\_t;A\_t \textbar{} S\_\{t\ensuremath{-}1\},A\_\{t\ensuremath{-}1\}) and a Bellman equation (Lucas 2026 Eq.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Polani-information01}: Relevant information uses maximum-entropy tie-breaking over optimal actions: a different selection rule from the H(A\textbar{}S)=0 regulator
\item \citet{Kolchinsky-semantic18}: Viability = -H(own state), with value -H(Z): the only information-theoretic action paper with our sign
\item \citet{Lucas-bellman26}: Plasticity Bellman equation (Eq 6); Thm A.2 restricts to deterministic policies by Bauer maximum principle; optimum has H(A\textbar{}S)=0: mirror of our vertex argument
\end{itemize}

\section{S4 Objectives on the occupancy measure, and how to optimise them}
404 papers in 11 nodes: 20 core, 106 support, 278 background; 34 families hold 347 papers.

\medskip\textsc{Naming, settled once for the whole cluster}

\subsection{2. S4/general-utility}\label{node:S4-S4-general-utility}
\textit{Merged from} S4/general-utility/maxent-state-entropy (39); S4/general-utility/pseudo-reward-algorithms (14); S4/general-utility/distribution-matching (6). \textbf{Relation} $-$/$\approx$: Opposite sign. Same template with F's sign flipped, so every global-convergence guarantee here is for the other sign. Two places touch our sign: Zahavy's non-convex remark, and DIAYN as convex maximisation of I(S;Z), Zahavy Sec. 6.2. \citet{Milosevic-dually26} writes the linearise-and-solve step with a KL prox, Eq. 20 to 21. As \ensuremath{\alpha} \ensuremath{\to} \ensuremath{\infty} that is our MM step, but its guarantee rests on relative smoothness for concave maximisation. [S4/general-utility/maxent-state-entropy] Opposite sign. Their optima are mixtures, interior points; ours are vertices. Sign-flipped, the negated kNN reward is a nonparametric ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}(z). APT with the sign reversed is our MM with \ensuremath{\hat{q}} refit every batch. The line splits into episodic and lifelong bonuses, and that split is our choice of \ensuremath{\hat{q}} scope. \citet{Henaff-exploration22} finds that state-entropy bonuses work through their per-episode count term, so on the MaxEnt side the working signal is E H(\ensuremath{\hat{d}}K), not H(d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}K). \citet{Li-element24} mixes the two explicitly. [S4/general-utility/pseudo-reward-algorithms] Same mechanism, different objective. The chain rule transfers: our MM inner step is the pseudo-reward MDP with reward E\_K ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}. The hidden-convexity global result does not transfer; for us stationary points are the basins. \citet{Barakat-global25} makes the split exact. Its gradient domination, Thm 1, and global rate, Thm 3, assume F concave. For our sign only the stationarity rate of Thm 2, O\ensuremath{\sim}(m \ensuremath{\varepsilon}\textsuperscript{$-$}\textsuperscript{2}), applies. A fitted pseudo-reward is also biased: with non-linear f, E[\ensuremath{\partial}f(\^J)] \ensuremath{\neq} \ensuremath{\partial}f(E \^J), and \citet{Ganesh-breaking26} corrects this with MLMC-NPG. Our ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}, fitted from finite samples, carries the same bias. [S4/general-utility/distribution-matching] Same set-point term, opposite entropy sign. Ours is min\_\ensuremath{\pi} min\_q, so alternation is monotone and averaging hurts, since it raises pooled H.

\textbf{Idea.} Maximise a concave F(d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}). It can be cast as a cost-player vs policy-player game, Zahavy, or as a potential MFG, Geist. The node also holds the expressivity results that motivate F(d) theory: scalar Markov rewards cannot express most multi-objective, risk and modal tasks, \citet{Skalse-limitations24}. Trajectory-level feedback, \citet{Chatterji-theory21} and \citet{Efroni-reinforcement20}, stays linear in the trajectory law, so it has no Jensen gap. Constrained variants are \citet{Agarwal-concave21} for CURL and \citet{Li-double24} for convex RL.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S4/general-utility/maxent-state-entropy [\ensuremath{-}]: Maximise state or observation entropy.
\item Absorbed S4/general-utility/pseudo-reward-algorithms [\ensuremath{\approx}]: Policy gradient via the chain rule \ensuremath{\nabla}\_\ensuremath{\theta} F(\ensuremath{\lambda}(\ensuremath{\theta})) = \ensuremath{\Sigma} \ensuremath{\partial}F/\ensuremath{\partial}\ensuremath{\lambda} \ensuremath{\nabla}\_\ensuremath{\theta} \ensuremath{\lambda}, with pseudo-reward \ensuremath{\partial}F/\ensuremath{\partial}\ensuremath{\lambda}.
\item Absorbed S4/general-utility/distribution-matching [\ensuremath{-}]: State-marginal matching, min KL(\ensuremath{\rho}\_\ensuremath{\pi}\ensuremath{\|}p*) = max E log p* + H.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Geist-concave22}: CURL named and cast as a potential mean-field game, monotone iff F concave; our sign gives an anti-monotone crowd-seeking MFG whose Nash equilibria are MM fixed points
\item \citet{Zahavy-reward21}: Convex MDPs as a cost-player/policy-player game; names the "convex MDP"; non-convex remark and DIAYN-as-convex-maximisation are the CURL line touching our sign
\item \citet{Hazan-provably19}: Founding MaxEnt-exploration theory: Frank-Wolfe over policy mixtures, smoothed entropy; sign-flipped sibling whose mixtures are interior points where ours are vertices
\item \citet{Zhang-variational20}: General-utility RL: policy gradient via pseudo-reward dF/dlambda, our Gibbs reward; hidden convexity global result is exactly what fails for our sign
\end{itemize}

\subsection{3. S4/concave-min, with 1 child}\label{node:S4-S4-concave-min}
\textit{Merged from} S4/occupancy-polytope (8); S4/concave-min/combinatorial-global (20). \textbf{Relation} $=$/$\subset$: Same objective class. \citet{Derman-finite70ch7} is the earliest MDP statement, for any continuous concave f of long-run frequencies; see node 1. \citet{Piunovskiy-controlled98}, Lemma 3 and Thm 4, gives the general form: any criterion concave under mixing admits a selector no worse than any policy. \citet{Collins-using97} treats a strictly convex \ensuremath{\phi} of the final-state law maximised: vertex, Markov deterministic optimum and best-response step. \citet{DeSanti-global24} is the same-sign RL template: supermodular trajectory rewards and semi-gradient MM. \citet{Touchette-information00} and 04, owned by S1, are the one-step control precedent. \citet{Prajapat-submodular24} is not same-sign. It maximises E\_\ensuremath{\tau} F(\ensuremath{\tau}) for a submodular coverage F, which is linear in the trajectory law. Its Prop 2 gets a deterministic Markov optimum for any set function through per-state multilinearity, a route to determinism that uses no concavity. [S4/occupancy-polytope] Same objective class at the root, and the tool we use. \citet{Derman-finite70ch7}, Thm 2, shows the closed convex hulls of frequencies over all policies and over deterministic stationary policies coincide. The Application after Thm 2 then proves that any continuous concave f of the long-run expected frequencies is minimised by a deterministic stationary policy. That is the vertex argument of \texttt{thm:goodreg} for a multi-step MDP, in 1970. Derman's Thm 3 needs stationary randomised policies once a constraint set is added. Thm 5 shows sample frequencies stay in the hull a.s., and for irreducible chains E liminf f(Z\_T) has the same minimum as f(X). [S4/concave-min/combinatorial-global] Special case, plus tools. With a deterministic read-out, our horizon-1 problem is weighted minimum-entropy set cover. \citet{Halperin-minimum05}, Sec. 3, derives it as joint maximum likelihood with p\_i = n\_i/N, which is our horizon-1 refit loop. H[dK] has nonlinear rank at most \textbar{}Z\textbar{} \ensuremath{-} 1. \citet{Horst-global90}, Ch. VIII, reduces a concave function of few variables to the projected polytope, so for small \textbar{}Z\textbar{} a certified global optimum is computable. The same reduction appears in RL form in \citet{Alegre-optimistic22}'s convex coverage set; see node 9.

\textbf{Idea.} A concave function minimised over an MDP polytope attains its minimum at a vertex, i.e. a deterministic policy. It has many local minima, and best-response linearisation is the natural local method.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S4/occupancy-polytope [=]: State-action frequencies of an MDP form a polytope whose vertices are deterministic stationary policies; linear objectives give LPs and linear constraints give CMDPs.
\item Absorbed S4/concave-min/combinatorial-global [\ensuremath{\subset}]: Hardness and exact methods for concave or entropy minimisation.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Collins-using97}: Refutes gap 6: vertex, Markov deterministic, optimum for convex phi of the final law, best-response linearisation = our MM inner step, outer-approximation global method
\item \citet{DeSanti-global24}: Closest same-sign algorithmic template: supermodular trajectory rewards, semi-gradient MM with monotone improvement, curvature-dependent hardness
\item \citet{Derman-finite70ch7}: Read (W4): Thm 2 hull of deterministic stationary frequencies; Application p.94 proves any continuous concave f of long-run frequencies is minimised by a deterministic stationary policy, our vertex theorem in 1970; Thm 5 sample frequencies for irreducible chains
\item \citet{Cardinal-minimum10}: Our horizon-1 problem with deterministic read-out is weighted minimum-entropy set cover: NP-hard, greedy within log e bits; ML-assignment form is our Gibbs MM
\end{itemize}

\subsection{3a. S4/concave-min/variance-occupancy}\label{node:S4-S4-concave-min-variance-occupancy}
\textit{Child of} \ref{node:S4-S4-concave-min}. \textbf{Relation} $\subset$: Special case, same mechanism. For Gaussian outcomes H = \ensuremath{\tfrac12} ln(2\ensuremath{\pi}e \ensuremath{\sigma}\textsuperscript{2}), so this is the Gaussian surrogate of our objective. Our frozen statistic is a density q, not a scalar y. \citet{Xia-optimization16} is the exact scalar twin. Its Lemma 1 gives \ensuremath{\eta}\_\{\ensuremath{\sigma},\ensuremath{\lambda}\} = \ensuremath{\eta}\_\ensuremath{\sigma} + (\ensuremath{\eta} \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\lambda})\textsuperscript{2}, just as \ensuremath{-}E ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}} = H(Z) + KL(p\ensuremath{\|}\ensuremath{\hat{q}}). Its Algorithm 1 is freeze, policy-iterate on (r \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\eta})\textsuperscript{2}, refit. Thm 4 gives a deterministic optimum when r is policy-independent. In its 3-state example the loop reaches one of two deterministic fixed points, \ensuremath{\eta}\_\ensuremath{\sigma} = 13.10 or the global 0.83, depending on the start. The deterministic result is older than Xia. \citet{Filar-variance89}, Cor 2.1 and 4.1, proves it for the average and discount-normalised cases. \citet{Sobel-meanvariance94}, Prop 2, puts the optimum at a vertex of X without a mean constraint. \citet{Huang-finding94} solves the pooled case by parametric LP, and with B = 0, D = 1, C = \ensuremath{-}h its program is exactly min H[dK] for \textbar{}Z\textbar{} = 2.

\textbf{Idea.} Minimise the steady-state or occupancy variance of reward, a concave function of d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}. Write Var = min\_y E(r \ensuremath{-} y)\textsuperscript{2}, freeze y, solve an ordinary MDP, refit y. This gives a deterministic optimum, and the refit/solve fixed points are "local optimal policies".

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Xia-optimization16}: Promoted: exact scalar twin of our MM; Lemma 1 pseudo-variance majoriser, Alg 1 freeze-solve-refit, Thm 4 deterministic optimum, Thm 5 local optimum, two deterministic fixed points 13.10 vs 0.83
\item \citet{Zhang-meanvariance21}: MVPI: deep-RL algorithmic twin of our MM loop for variance (refit y, solve MDP with reward r - lambda r\^{}2 + 2 lambda r y), monotone improvement
\item \citet{Xia-global26}: Variance-of-occupancy precedent that narrows gap 1: concave occupancy functional minimised via frozen-mean pseudo-variance, deterministic optimality proof, refit/solve fixed points = local optima
\end{itemize}

\subsection{4. S4/finite-trial}\label{node:S4-S4-finite-trial}
\textit{Merged from} S4/finite-trial/single-life-ergodicity (14). \textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition and limitation. It decides which H(Z) our MM loop targets. With \ensuremath{\hat{q}} pooled across episodes the loop targets F(d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}K), the Conant--Ashby ensemble entropy. With \ensuremath{\hat{q}} refit per episode it targets E H(\ensuremath{\hat{d}}K), which is lower by I(Z; trial). By Mannor05 Thm 4.1 a weakly communicating regulator can drive I(Z; trial) to zero at its chosen vertex, even in multichain cases. [S4/finite-trial/single-life-ergodicity] Precondition and limitation. The gap I(Z; closed class) is specific to our sign: it appears exactly when a regulator can lock into one of several closed classes, basins. It is zero at a deterministic unichain fixed point. Single-life RL itself \citep{Chen-you22} has a linear objective and so no Jensen gap.

\textbf{Idea.} F(E \ensuremath{\hat{d}}\_n) differs from E F(\ensuremath{\hat{d}}\_n) whenever F is non-linear. For concave F, Jensen gives E F(\ensuremath{\hat{d}}\_n) \ensuremath{\leq} F(d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}). Other results in this node:
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item the error of using the infinite-trial optimum is O(LT\ensuremath{\surd}(S/n)) for L-Lipschitz F;
\item single-trial optima are deterministic and non-Markovian, and the problem is NP-hard;
\item the single trial is an MDP on, state, running occupancy, and a risk measure ERM\_\ensuremath{\beta} over the random single-trial occupancy is solved on the same augmented MDP, \citet{Santos-riskaware26}, Thms 4.5 and 4.6;
\item in the average-reward setting the gap vanishes for unichain chains and persists for multichain ones;
\item empirical frequencies of any history-dependent policy concentrate on the polytope, P(dist(q\_t, Q) \ensuremath{\geq} \ensuremath{\varepsilon}) \ensuremath{\leq} c\textsubscript{0}e\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}c\textsubscript{1}\ensuremath{\varepsilon}\textsuperscript{2}t\}, and under weak communication any occupancy is the a.s. limit of a single run, \citet{Mannor-empirical05}, Thms 5.1 and 4.1.
\item Absorbed S4/finite-trial/single-life-ergodicity [L]: Time average vs ensemble average: ergodicity economics, single-life and reset-free RL, and expectation vs satisfaction semantics of mean payoff.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Mutti-challenging22}: Defines finite-trial vs infinite-trial convex RL; Thm 4.1 bounds the loss of the infinite-trial optimum under n trials by 4LT sqrt(2S log(4T/delta)/n), proof sign-agnostic; user-flagged central to how MM is approximated
\item \citet{Mutti-convex23}: Journal version: Jensen gap zeta\_n \textless{}= zeta\_inf for concave F, Thm 1, deterministic non-Markovian single-trial optimum for any F, Lemma 1, NP-hardness, Thm 4; user-flagged; its gap is I(Z;trial) for our entropy
\item \citet{Santos-number25}: Infinite-horizon trials gap: zero for unichain average occupancy, Thm 4.4, persistent for multichain with Var[Ber(alpha\_l)] lower bound, Thm 4.6; decides ensemble vs single-life H(Z) for basins
\item \citet{Santos-solving26}: Single-trial GUMDP is an MDP on, state, running occupancy, deterministic policies suffice, NP-hard even for smooth f; finite-trial analogue of our belief-state augmentation
\end{itemize}

\subsection{5. S4/estimator-bias}\label{node:S4-S4-estimator-bias}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition and limitation. The policy-dependent part of the bias decides the argmin. In-sample plug-in is optimistic for a minimiser and flatters spread policies. Held-out E[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}] is exactly the MM surrogate and a valid certificate, with a distribution-free CI when \ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}} \ensuremath{\leq} F\_max; lower bounds are capped at ln(2kN\textsuperscript{2}). A \ensuremath{\kappa} floor on \ensuremath{\hat{q}} is required, since an unsmoothed \ensuremath{\hat{q}} gives \ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\infty} on unseen outcomes and locks the loop into its first basin. The floor keeps the loop stable; it does not remove bias. A Dirichlet-smoothed plug-in does not in general beat the MLE on bias, \citet{Han-does15}. A fixed add-\ensuremath{\kappa} prior also pins the implied entropy near \ensuremath{\xi}(\ensuremath{\beta}) = \ensuremath{\psi}\textsubscript{0}(K\ensuremath{\beta} + 1) \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\psi}\textsubscript{0}(\ensuremath{\beta} + 1), with width \ensuremath{\sim}1/\ensuremath{\surd}K, \citet{Nemenman-entropy01}, Eq. 8. So when N \ensuremath{\leq} K the floor acts as a strong prior on H itself; the NSB mixture over \ensuremath{\beta}, Eq. 11, is the fix.

\textbf{Idea.} Finite-sample entropy estimators and the sign of their bias.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Plug-in bias is \ensuremath{\approx} \ensuremath{-}(m\ensuremath{-}1)/2N, negative everywhere and O(1) when N \ensuremath{\sim} m.
\item Parametric in-sample optimism is \ensuremath{\approx} k/2N.
\item Held-out cross-entropy is an upper bound on H.
\item kNN bias is negative in the interior of smooth densities and positive at hard edges.
\item Bias is unbounded on manifolds, and Markov samples shrink the effective N. For a mean under the discounted kernel the lower bound is 1/\ensuremath{\surd}(N(1 \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\beta}\ensuremath{\gamma})), \citet{Metelli-tale23}, Thm 4.1. For plug-in entropy the Markov term \ensuremath{-}(1/N) \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_\{i\ensuremath{\geq}2\} \ensuremath{\lambda}\_i/(1 \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\lambda}\_i) depends on the spectral gap, \citet{DeGregorio-entropy23}, Eq. 33, so slow-mixing policies look better to an in-sample minimiser.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Paninski-estimation03}: Plug-in entropy is negatively biased, about -(m-1)/2N and uncorrectable when N \ensuremath{\sim} m; makes in-sample H(Z) optimistic for a minimiser and flatters spread policies
\item \citet{McAllester-formal20}: Cross-entropy E[-ln q] gives distribution-free upper bounds on H while lower bounds are capped at ln(2kN\^{}2): the certificate our sign needs is the easy one
\end{itemize}

\subsection{6. S4/mm-cccp}\label{node:S4-S4-mm-cccp}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{T}: Tool we use. It certifies that H(p\_k) converges to a KKT value up to the accumulated \ensuremath{\Sigma} KL(p\_k\ensuremath{\|}\ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_k). Because g is polyhedral, \citet{LeThi-dc18} property (v) gives a finite sequence and finite convergence to a critical point. Stopping at the first equal objective value prevents cycling among tied vertices, so the v1 requirement of strict convexity is not needed. Its local-minimiser clause needs h polyhedral, and our h is smooth, so that clause does not transfer. Finite is not small: the submodular--supermodular MM procedure can need exponentially many iterations and can stop at a non-global local optimum, \citet{Byrnes-tight15}. Our objective is analytic on the polytope, so the Kurdyka--\L{}ojasiewicz argument of \citet{Kang-global15} gives convergence of the iterates themselves, with rates. Yuille's CCCP theorem covers linear equality constraints only, which is why DCA, not CCCP, is the citable form for a polytope.

\textbf{Idea.} MM, EM, CCCP and DCA. Our loop is DCA with g = \ensuremath{\chi}\_D and h = \ensuremath{-}H\_\ensuremath{\mu}(dK). Each DCA step is one LP over the polytope, argmin\_\{d\ensuremath{\in}D\} \ensuremath{\Sigma} d(s,a) E\_K[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_k(z)], which is our frozen-\ensuremath{\hat{q}} MDP solve. Zangwill's theory gives convergence to KKT points. Partial inner solves suffice (GEM, \citet{Dempster-maximum77}), and BSUM and stochastic MM cover inexact steps. Hard-assignment EM is vertex-valued MM: CEM's refitted proportion term is \ensuremath{-}nH(cluster sizes), and its criterion is linear in the fuzzy assignment, so its optimum is a hard partition, \citet{Celeux-classification92}, Eq. 3.6.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{LeThi-dc18}: Promoted: our loop is DCA with g the polytope indicator, polyhedral, and h = -H\_mu(dK); property (v) gives finite convergence to a KKT point with no strict-convexity assumption; CCCP and EM are DCA
\end{itemize}

\subsection{7. S4/refit-loops, with 2 children}\label{node:S4-S4-refit-loops}
\textit{Merged from} S4/refit-loops/model-collapse (16); S4/refit-loops/self-referential-rewards (5). \textbf{Relation} $\approx$/$\sim$: Same mechanism, different objective. Parent members are \citet{Monderer-potential96} and \citet{Robinson-iterative51}. [S4/refit-loops/model-collapse] Same sign, from sampling error. In finite-sample MM both effects act. The disturbance plays the role of the real-data fraction. This makes the \ensuremath{\kappa} floor, a Dirichlet prior and a pooled \ensuremath{\hat{q}} design requirements. Chen25 is the formal case for the floor; Barzilai25 means the pooled \ensuremath{\hat{q}} alone does not guarantee it. [S4/refit-loops/self-referential-rewards] Same mechanism. For exact log-loss refit the parameter-steering term vanishes by the envelope theorem at \ensuremath{\hat{q}} = d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}K, so our loop is the exception in which refit plus best response is exact descent, versus \citet{Gauthier-explaining26}. Skew-Fit's unique fixed point holds for the MaxEnt sign only.

\textbf{Idea.} A model is refit to data generated by a policy that optimises against it. Our loop is alternating best response in the two-player identical-payoff game \ensuremath{\Phi}(\ensuremath{\pi}, q) = E ln q, an exact potential game, \citet{Monderer-potential96}. It therefore descends monotonically up to KL(p\ensuremath{\|}\ensuremath{\hat{q}}) and has the finite improvement property, with no guarantee of a global optimum.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S4/refit-loops/model-collapse [\ensuremath{\sim}]: Generative models refit on their own samples lose entropy and are absorbed in point masses.
\item Absorbed S4/refit-loops/self-referential-rewards [\ensuremath{\approx}]: RL rewards computed from a density of the agent's own visitation or from a reward model refit during training: Skew-Fit, SMiRL, DeRL and iterated RLHF.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Family S4.F22} model collapse under self-consumption, 13 papers: {\footnotesize \citetext{\citealp{Shumailov-curse23}; \citealp{Bertrand-stability23}; \citealp{Barzilai-when25}; \citealp{Chen-entropyreservoir25}; \citealp{Alemohammad-selfconsuming23}; \citealp{Dey-universality24}; \citealp{Dohmatob-model24}; \citealp{Gerstgrasser-model24}; \citealp{Gillman-selfcorrecting24}; \citealp{Fu-theoretical25}; \citealp{Kanabar-model25}; \citealp{Suresh-rate24}; \citealp{Liu-oligarch26}}}

\subsection{7a. S4/refit-loops/performative}\label{node:S4-S4-refit-loops-performative}
\textit{Child of} \ref{node:S4-S4-refit-loops}. \textbf{Relation} $\approx$: Same mechanism. PR(q) = E\_\{p\_\{\ensuremath{\pi}(q)\}\}[\ensuremath{-}ln q]: the PO is our global minimum and the PS points are our MM fixed points. The Gibbs reward is performatively exact, so PS points coincide with KKT points of H(dK). The uniqueness results need \ensuremath{\varepsilon}-sensitivity or a strongly concave regulariser, which our discontinuous best response lacks. Prytula26 is the continuous-time picture of our loop, a policy coupled to its own state law.

\textbf{Idea.} Performative prediction and performative RL. Repeated retraining converges to performatively stable (PS) points, and PS points are not in general performative optima (PO). Other results in this node:
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item time-averaged no-regret refits are stable, Farina;
\item retraining seeks a model-independent stable signal, Hardt;
\item Hedge is locally stable under discontinuous maps \citep{Mandal-local26};
\item finding stable points is PPAD-hard;
\item joint actor, critic and state-law dynamics have global attractors and a fast-slow reduction, \citet{Prytula-global26}.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Perdomo-performative20}: Performative prediction: stable points of repeated risk minimisation = our MM fixed points, performative optimum = global min; its uniqueness theorems fail for our discontinuous best response
\end{itemize}

\subsection{7b. S4/refit-loops/misspecified-fixed-points}\label{node:S4-S4-refit-loops-misspecified-fixed-points}
\textit{Child of} \ref{node:S4-S4-refit-loops}. \textbf{Relation} $\approx$: Same mechanism, different objective. Our MM fixed point has the shape of a Berk--Nash equilibrium of an MDP (\citet{Esponda-equilibrium21} Def. 8), but there the belief is over transitions and the payoff is fixed, while our \ensuremath{\hat{q}} sets the payoff. This supplies existence, stability and escape-rate theory. Zhu26's LPs do not transfer verbatim, since its model enters the kernel. Its bilevel program selects least-KL equilibria, which with a full-simplex \ensuremath{\hat{q}} is every MM fixed point. Our MM is joint coordinate descent, a stronger property than Berk--Nash. \citet{Tschantz-learning20} shows the failure mode in a tabular POMDP: 36\% of instrumental-only agents never learn to chemotax, a self-confirming fixed point of a greedy policy with a learned model.

\textbf{Idea.} Self-confirming and Berk--Nash equilibria are fixed points where the model is right only on the agent's own path. Certainty-equivalence adaptive control has the same shape: estimate, act optimally, re-estimate. It yields closed-loop identification only, is biased toward lower reward, and has an optimism fix in RBMLE. \citet{Zhu-mathematical26} computes Berk--Nash equilibria of infinite-horizon MDPs by coupled occupancy LPs and a bilevel program with entropy smoothing.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Esponda-equilibrium21}: Berk-Nash equilibrium of an MDP (Def. 8) is structurally our MM fixed point; supplies existence, stability and escape-dynamics theory
\end{itemize}

\subsection{8. S4/deterministic-scope}\label{node:S4-S4-deterministic-scope}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition and limitation of the deterministic-optimum claim once learning enters. State the theorem as Bayes-optimality given the prior. Our pooled H is non-separable, so the Whittle relaxation does not apply. Bastani21 is the favourable case for us: the disturbance D can supply the exploration that a deterministic regulator does not.

\textbf{Idea.} Deterministic Bayes-optimal policies need not be asymptotically optimal. Greedy or certainty-equivalent policies fail if and only if a decoy exists. Greedy is rate-optimal when the context carries enough randomness, \citet{Bastani-mostly21}: exogenous variation stands in for exploration. Some deterministic policies are nonetheless frequentist-good: finite-horizon Gittins, Bayes-UCB, RBMLE. Whittle indices exist for separable concave belief costs.

\subsection{9. S4/occupancy-models}\label{node:S4-S4-occupancy-models}
\textit{Merged from} S4/occupancy-models/correction-and-solvers (28). \textbf{Relation} \textsf{T}: Tool we use.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Each MM step needs only an embedding z\_k = E\_\ensuremath{\rho}[ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_k(Z) B(Z)], with no new RL, when ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}} lies in the span of B.
\item With an exponential-family \ensuremath{\hat{q}}, the Gibbs reward is linear in E\_K \ensuremath{\phi}. The critic is then an SF, and GPI over past iterates is a safe MM step.
\item For our sign, plain deterministic FB contains the optimum.
\item The distributional successor measure \citep{Wiltzer-distributional24} makes I(Z; trial) estimable.
\item **Note, from \citet{Alegre-optimistic22}, recorded here rather than as a child node.** Take \ensuremath{\phi} = K(\ensuremath{\cdot}\textbar{}s,a), so \ensuremath{\psi} = dK/(1 \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\gamma}). Our objective is concave in \ensuremath{\psi}, so its minimiser is a point of the convex coverage set, the CCS. SFOLS, Alg. 1 and Thm 3.2, enumerates a finite candidate set that provably contains it, and each MM step with w = ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}} lands on the CCS. This is the RL form of the low-nonlinear-rank global method of node 3b. It is one paper, so it stays a note. [S4/occupancy-models/correction-and-solvers] Tool we use.
\item DICE removes the replay-mixture slack KL(p\_k\ensuremath{\|}\ensuremath{\hat{q}}) when fitting \ensuremath{\hat{q}} to the current iterate.
\item Strong duality fails for the outer concave minimisation, which is why DICE entropy methods exist only for the MaxEnt sign.
\item Contrastive RL estimates I(S,A; Z\textsubscript{+}), not the marginal entropy we score.
\item Average-reward solvers are needed for the single-life reading.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Learned representations of d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} for many policies:
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item the successor representation (SR);
\item successor features (SF) with generalised policy improvement, GPI, over stored policies, including universal SFs;
\item forward-backward (FB) zero-shot models;
\item zero-shot general utility, FB-IL, Soft FB;
\item generative occupancy models, \ensuremath{\gamma}-models, TD-flows, and the distributional successor measure;
\item goal-conditioned survival values, a discounted sum of survival probabilities to a goal, \citet{NguimatsiaTiofack-survival26} and -svl26.
\item Absorbed S4/occupancy-models/correction-and-solvers [T]: Supporting machinery:
\item DICE stationary-distribution correction and DICE policy optimisation;
\item contrastive density-ratio occupancy estimators;
\item average-reward deep RL;
\item value functions over a changing preference vector.
\end{itemize}

\section{S5 Reward through channels, uncertainty and belief}
316 papers in 12 nodes: 18 core, 92 support, 206 background; 19 families hold 169 papers.

\medskip\textsc{reward-as-probability}

\subsection{S5/reward-as-probability/linear-likelihood}\label{node:S5-S5-reward-as-probability-linear-likelihood}
\textit{Merged from} S5/reward-as-probability/target-utility (16); S5/reward-as-probability/corrupted-channels (13). \textbf{Relation} $\subset$/\textsf{C}: Special case. This is literally the "good" emission of K\_\ensuremath{\rho}, and Toussaint's P(R;\ensuremath{\pi}) is our R. Two members depart from the plain linear form.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item **\citet{Tajwar-maximum26}** maximises E\_x ln p\_\ensuremath{\theta}(x), with the log inside the average over contexts. By Jensen E\_x ln p \ensuremath{\leq} ln E\_x p, and the gradient reweights hard contexts by 1/p(x). So "log P(good) has the same argmax as R" holds per context only, not across contexts.
\item **\citet{Peters-reinforcement07}** uses u\_\ensuremath{\tau}(r) = \ensuremath{\tau} e\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\tau}r\} and refits \ensuremath{\tau} by weighted ML on the agent's own reweighted outcomes, Eq 18. That is a one-parameter version of our \ensuremath{\hat{q}} refit.
\item **\citet{Strupl-reward21}** proves that reward-weighted regression, an EM freeze-then-refit loop, converges to the global optimum, R-linearly in finite MDPs, without function approximation. It is the nearest template for a convergence claim about our MM loop. Read at abstract level. We minimise F(R), for which the scale matters through N. [S5/reward-as-probability/target-utility] Special case. It gives the normative reason for requiring r \ensuremath{\in} [0,1] and reading it as a probability.
\item Castagnoli--LiCalzi Sec. 7 lets the benchmark depend on the lottery, Pr(X \ensuremath{\geq} V) = \ensuremath{\int}U(x;F)dF. \citet{Abbas-normative05} gives a second instance, an aspiration equivalent F(\#x) = \ensuremath{\int}F dU with EU = Pr(X \textgreater{} \#x), Eq 8. Its benchmark moves with the lottery but is fixed by U. Neither sets the benchmark equal to F itself.
\item \citet{White-minimizing93}, Thm 1, and \citet{Bouakiz-targetlevel95}, Thm 4.5, find a deterministic stationary optimum only on the state augmented with the running level, W\_\{t+1\} = (W\_t \ensuremath{-} Y\_t)/\ensuremath{\rho}. Determinism holds on (s, r), not on s. This is the target-level form of the augmentation our \ensuremath{\hat{q}} avoids by freezing.
\item Kolobov's iSSPUDE breaks ties lexicographically over P*-greedy actions, which mirrors choosing among optimal vertices. [S5/reward-as-probability/corrupted-channels] Contrast. These papers invert or defend a channel to recover the mean reward. We fix a known symmetric confusion channel and score the entropy of its output. If the agent could steer the noise, an entropy objective would seek low-entropy corrupted states. Uniform noise makes the noise basin benign. Skalse et al. show that unhackability hinges on reward being linear in visit counts: over all stochastic policies only trivial reward pairs are unhackable, while over deterministic policies nontrivial pairs exist. Our objective is concave, not linear, in the occupancy, and its optimum is a deterministic vertex, so the deterministic-policy case is the relevant one. Read at abstract level.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Rescale reward to [0,1], read it as the probability of a binary success event, and maximise the probability of that event. With a geometric horizon, L\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} = P(r=1; \ensuremath{\pi}) = (1\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\gamma})V\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}. Cooper 1988 already rescales reward to P(V=T\textbar{}\ensuremath{\Pi}) = (v + k\textsubscript{2})/k\textsubscript{1}, and maximises P(good) itself, not its log.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S5/reward-as-probability/target-utility [\ensuremath{\subset}]: Any bounded utility is the CDF of an independent benchmark V, so expected utility equals Pr(X \ensuremath{\geq} V).
\item Absorbed S5/reward-as-probability/corrupted-channels [C]: The observed reward is the output of a corrupted channel.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Toussaint-probabilistic06}: Direct ancestor of K\_rho: binary reward event with P(r=1)=rescaled reward, likelihood, 1-gamma,V\^{}pi, Thm 3.1; they maximise log P(good), we minimise entropy of the full emitted law
\item \citet{Castagnoli-expected96}: Decision-theoretic twin of K\_rho: E u(X)=Pr(X\textgreater{}=V); Sec. 7 lottery-dependent benchmark is the closest analogue of our self-refitted q
\end{itemize}

\subsection{S5/reward-as-probability/entropy-as-risk}\label{node:S5-S5-reward-as-probability-entropy-as-risk}
\textbf{Relation} $=$: Same objective, one step or open loop. Allahverdyan's Eq. 18 is our H + E[c] where the outcome is the action and there are no dynamics. Adurthi carries it through dynamics, but u is fixed before \ensuremath{\rho} is revealed, so there is no feedback and no belief update.

\textbf{Idea.} In decision theory, outcome entropy is a risk measure.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Allahverdyan et al. minimise \ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\Sigma}p ln p + \ensuremath{\hat{\beta}} \ensuremath{\Sigma} p \ensuremath{\varepsilon} over a polytope and get vertex local minima, frustration, and \ensuremath{\varepsilon}-greedy as a local optimum.
\item Yang--Qiu \textbf{posit} the risk measure R(a) = \ensuremath{\lambda}H\_a(\ensuremath{\theta}) \ensuremath{-} (1\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\lambda})E u / max\textbar{}E u\textbar{}, Def. 1, and choose the pure action argmin R(a), Eq 14. Their entropy is of the state of nature given the action, H(Z\textbar{}A=a), and for a pure action I(A;Z) = 0, so this is H(Z). The \ensuremath{\lambda} = 1 case is credited to Luce 1980.
\item Luce et al. 2008 axiomatise U(g) = SEU(g) + [h(\ensuremath{\Omega}) \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\Sigma} h(C\_i)]/\ensuremath{\mu}(\ensuremath{\Omega}), Thm 13(i), with a general branching term h. The Shannon form, entropy of degree \ensuremath{\alpha} at \ensuremath{\alpha} = 1, appears only in the companion Utility of gambling II. The sign of the coefficient is free: a liking for gambling is the opposite sign to ours.
\item **\citet{Adurthi-minimum14}** minimises \ensuremath{\lambda}H(J) + (1\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\lambda})E[J] over open-loop bang-bang controls of \ensuremath{\dot{x}} = A(\ensuremath{\rho})x + B(\ensuremath{\rho})u under a parameter prior \ensuremath{\rho} \ensuremath{\sim} p(\ensuremath{\rho}), Eq 3. Dividing by \ensuremath{\lambda}, H(J) + ((1\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\lambda})/\ensuremath{\lambda}) E[J] = H\_\ensuremath{\mu}(J),  \ensuremath{\mu}(dJ) = e\^{}\{((1\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\lambda})/\ensuremath{\lambda}) J\} dJ, which is our reference-measure objective with Z = J and the parameter as the disturbance. The authors call it non-convex, with local minima, and give no theorem.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Adurthi-minimum14}: min lambda H(J)+(1-lambda)E J = H\_mu(J) with mu=e\^{}\{((1-lambda)/lambda)J\}: our reference-measure objective through dynamics, open loop at the prior; the sequential instance the node lacked; moved from gap-2 probes
\item \citet{Allahverdyan-adaptive18}: Same objective in one-shot form: min H+beta*E[cost] over a polytope, vertex local minima, MaxEnt as the risk-seeking dual; nearest prior statement of our sign
\end{itemize}
\begin{multicols}{2}
\memb{Other support, 4.} \citetext{\citealp{Yang-measure05}; \citealp{Luce-utility08}; \citealp{Yang-normalized14}; \citealp{Chiew-performance21}}

\memb{Other background, 3.} {\footnotesize \citetext{\citealp{Luce-utility08b}\dag; \citealp{Brito-decision20}; \citealp{Brito-normalized22}\dag}}

\medskip\textsc{convex-twins}

\subsection{S5/convex-twins/exponential-kl-inference}\label{node:S5-S5-convex-twins-exponential-kl-inference}
\textit{Merged from} S5/convex-twins/maxent-policy (16). \textbf{Relation} $-$: Opposite sign, same structure. Our H\_\ensuremath{\mu} = H + E[c] mirrors q + KL, but our information term is concave, so the optimum is a deterministic vertex, not a tilted stochastic law.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Todorov 2007 writes KL = \ensuremath{-}H + cross-entropy, so the next-state entropy enters with the opposite sign to ours.
\item Kappen 2005's double slit shows delayed choice below \ensuremath{\nu}T = 1, Eq 34, the opposite of early commitment.
\item \textbf{VAPOR drops the e\^{}r surrogate.} Tarbouriech et al. maximise \ensuremath{\lambda}\textsuperscript{T} E r + H\_\ensuremath{\tau}(\ensuremath{\lambda}) with the weighted occupancy entropy H\_\ensuremath{\tau}(\ensuremath{\lambda}) = \ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\Sigma} \ensuremath{\tau} \ensuremath{\lambda} log \ensuremath{\lambda}, Eqs 2--4. So VAPOR is opposite sign on the \textbf{occupancy} entropy, the same kind of object as ours, not on the policy entropy. Their Lemma 8, E[\ensuremath{\lambda}\_TS] = P(\ensuremath{\Gamma}*), makes the mean occupancy of Thompson sampling a mixture of deterministic vertices. The O'Donoghue critique supports putting the posterior into the state. [S5/convex-twins/maxent-policy] Opposite sign. Their entropy is of the action given the state, or of the path, and they maximise it. Ours is of the outcome marginal, and our budget penalises I(A;Z\textbar{}S). Gr\"unwald's H = min\_q E[\ensuremath{-}ln q] is the game we play the other way. \citet{Jhaveri-convergence25} shows that vanishing entropy regularisation selects the maximum-entropy optimal policy, the opposite tie-break to our deterministic vertex. SMiRL, owned by B5, is the sign-flipped sibling.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} The optimality likelihood p(O=1\textbar{}s,a) = e\^{}r gives the risk-seeking backup log E e\^{}V under exact inference, and reward + H(\ensuremath{\pi}) under the variational fix. The same object appears as KL control, with cost q(x) + KL(u\ensuremath{\|}p), a linear Bellman equation in z = e\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}v\}, and the tilted path law q\ensuremath{\cdot}e\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\Sigma}R\}/Z. Theodorou's duality gives \ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\lambda} log E\_P e\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}J/\ensuremath{\lambda}\} = inf\_Q E\_Q J + \ensuremath{\lambda} KL(Q\ensuremath{\|}P). O'Donoghue et al. show that the surrogate "probability of optimality" ignores epistemic uncertainty and fails on DeepSea. K-learning repairs this inside the e\^{}r frame.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S5/convex-twins/maxent-policy [\ensuremath{-}]: Maximise r + \ensuremath{\alpha} H(\ensuremath{\pi}(\ensuremath{\cdot}\textbar{}s)).
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Todorov-efficient09}: KL control: state cost + KL to passive dynamics, convex, tilted stochastic optimum; opposite-sign structural twin of H\_mu = H + E[c]
\item \citet{Levine-reinforcement18}: Control as inference: exponential optimality likelihood, +H(pi), stochastic optimum; the contrast every reviewer will expect
\item \citet{ODonoghue-making20}: RL-as-inference ignores epistemic uncertainty and fails on DeepSea; supports putting the posterior in the state; K-learning baseline
\item \citet{Tarbouriech-probabilistic23}: VAPOR maximises weighted occupancy entropy H\_tau(lambda) of d\^{}pi, the opposite sign on our kind of object; Lemma 8 E[lambda\_TS]=P(Gamma*) makes TS a mixture of deterministic vertices; DeepSea baseline [tier changed from support]
\end{itemize}

\subsection{S5/convex-twins/return-law-risk}\label{node:S5-S5-convex-twins-return-law-risk}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{C}: Contrast, with one exception. On K\_\ensuremath{\rho}, F depends only on the mean return, so the threshold is not a risk attitude.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \textbf{Exception: the occupancy-variance line}, \citet{Filar-variance89} and its descendants. Variance of the reward under state-action frequencies is a concave function of the occupancy, and G's gap 1 shows it is minimised by the freeze-refit-solve loop: Xia, and MVPI with Var = min\_y E(R\ensuremath{-}y)\textsuperscript{2}. That member is \textbf{same mechanism, different objective}, the Gaussian stand-in for our entropy.
\item \textbf{Not the exception: Sobel's return variance.} V = (I \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\beta}\textsuperscript{2}P)\textsuperscript{$-$}\textsuperscript{1}\ensuremath{\theta}, \ensuremath{\theta}\_s = \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_j p\_sj(r\_sj + \ensuremath{\beta}v\_j)\textsuperscript{2} \ensuremath{-} v\_s\textsuperscript{2}, Thm 1. It is not a function of the occupancy. Sec 4 gives a counterexample to monotonicity, so policy iteration on E B \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\lambda}\ensuremath{\surd}Var B may fail. It is a contrast.
\item \citet{Wang-reductions24} solves OCE risks inf\_y \{y + E u(G \ensuremath{-} y)\}, covering CVaR, entropic risk and mean-variance, by putting y in the state. That is the return-law analogue of our frozen q, and it generalises the MVPI member. Read at abstract level.
\item \textbf{Caveat.} \citet{Jaquette-utility76} shows that exponential-utility MDPs can have optimal policies that are not stationary. Nonlinear return-law criteria can break stationary deterministic optimality, so the vertex argument holds for functionals of the occupancy, not of the return law. Howard 1972 proves policy-iteration convergence only over stationary deterministic policies, App. B.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Functionals of the return law: exponential utility (1/\ensuremath{\beta}) log E e\^{}\{\ensuremath{\beta}G\}, variance-penalised MDPs, coherent risk, CVaR, optimised certainty equivalents, and distributional RL, which learns the law of G itself.

\medskip\textsc{targets-and-scalars}

\subsection{S5/targets-and-scalars/outcome-targets}\label{node:S5-S5-targets-and-scalars-outcome-targets}
\textit{Merged from} S5/targets-and-scalars/satisficing (10); S5/targets-and-scalars/reward-expressivity (11). \textbf{Relation} $\subset$/\textsf{C}/\textsf{L}: Special case of our MM step. The MM reward E\_K ln q with q frozen at a target \ensuremath{\delta}\_g is ODRL's reward. We refit q to the policy's own outcome law and seek a self-consistent fixed point. \citet{Eysenbach-imitating22} shows that imitating one's own relabelled outcomes is a fixed-point loop that need not improve the policy. Our loop is monotone because the surrogate majorises the objective. For any \ensuremath{\hat{q}}, H(d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K) = min\_q E\_\{d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K\}[\ensuremath{-}ln q] \ensuremath{\leq} E\_\{d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K\}[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}], with equality at \ensuremath{\hat{q}} = d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K. So with \ensuremath{\pi}\_\{t+1\} = argmin\_\ensuremath{\pi} E\_\{d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K\}[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_t] and \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_t = d\^{}\{\ensuremath{\pi}\_t\}K, H(d\^{}\{\ensuremath{\pi}\_\{t+1\}\}K) \ensuremath{\leq} E\_\{d\^{}\{\ensuremath{\pi}\_\{t+1\}\}K\}[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_t] \ensuremath{\leq} E\_\{d\^{}\{\ensuremath{\pi}\_t\}K\}[\ensuremath{-}ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}\_t] = H(d\^{}\{\ensuremath{\pi}\_t\}K). Outcome-conditioned imitation fits \ensuremath{\pi} to relabelled data and has no such majoriser. [S5/targets-and-scalars/satisficing] Contrast. Simon 1955 uses a binary pay-off V(s) \ensuremath{\in} \{0,1\} and a rule that finds an action whose outcome set lies inside the satisfactory set, which is the worst-case support inclusion of our viability box. His aspiration levels move with how easily satisfactory options are found. Our viability box also gives satisficing, but the entropy term then selects the deterministic, most regular feasible vertex. Dima randomises to reach the interior, and quantilisers keep the policy spread on purpose. [S5/targets-and-scalars/reward-expressivity] Precondition or limitation. H[d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} K] is concave in mixtures of occupancies, violates independence, and so lies outside the Markov Reward Theorem. On K\_\ensuremath{\rho} it re-enters: fully where R \textgreater{} 1/(N+1), and at the argmin whenever h\_b(R*) \textless{} R* ln N. In Subramani's preorder our objective is a nonlinear function of the occupancy measure. Where that class sits relative to Markov reward needs the full text, which was not read.

\textbf{Idea.} Replace reward by a desired outcome. Variational outcome-driven RL derives the reward log p\_d(g\textbar{}s,a). The node also covers goal-conditioned RL, hindsight relabelling, return-conditioned policies such as upside-down RL and the Decision Transformer, quasimetric goal-reaching values, and example-based policy search.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S5/targets-and-scalars/satisficing [C]: Meet an aspiration level rather than maximise.
\item Absorbed S5/targets-and-scalars/reward-expressivity [L]: Which preferences a Markov reward can express.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Rudner-outcomedriven21}: Outcome-driven RL reward log p\_d(g\textbar{}s,a) is our MM reward E\_K ln q with q frozen at a target; ours is the self-consistent fixed point
\item \citet{Bowling-settling23}: Markov Reward Theorem needs vNM independence; H[d\^{}pi K] is concave in mixtures so lies outside it, re-entering on K\_rho for R\textgreater{}1/(N+1)
\end{itemize}
\begin{multicols}{2}
\memb{Other support, 4.} \citetext{\citealp{Eysenbach-imitating22}; \citealp{Simon-behavioral55}; \citealp{Taylor-quantilizers16}; \citealp{Dima-nonmaximizing24}}

\medskip\textsc{belief-as-state}

\subsection{S5/belief-as-state/sufficiency}\label{node:S5-S5-belief-as-state-sufficiency}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition, with a scope limit. \AA{}str\"om proves sufficiency for a cost E \ensuremath{\Sigma} g(u, x\_t) that is linear in the state law, via V\_k(w) = min\_u[\ensuremath{\langle}g(u),w\ensuremath{\rangle} + \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_j \ensuremath{\|}z\_j\ensuremath{\|} V\_\{k+1\}(z\_j/\ensuremath{\|}z\_j\ensuremath{\|})]. Smallwood--Sondik's PWLC proof, V\_n = max\_k \ensuremath{\alpha}\^{}k\ensuremath{\cdot}\ensuremath{\pi}, Eq 7, also uses an objective linear in \ensuremath{\pi}. Our pooled entropy is not of that form. Sufficiency is restored by the fixed-q MM surrogate \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_z \ensuremath{\bar{K}}(z\textbar{}b,a) ln q(z), which is linear in b. Singh 1994 is the off-Markov counterexample to the vertex argument. Subramanian's condition says an information state must predict the whole outcome law, not only its mean. Papadimitriou--Tsitsiklis 1987 says that the MM surrogate, although an ordinary POMDP, inherits PSPACE-hardness at finite horizon.

\textbf{Idea.} The posterior is a sufficient statistic, and a POMDP is an MDP on beliefs with a PWLC value. Memoryless policies may need randomisation and lose arbitrarily much. An information state must predict reward and its own next value. The belief route has a price: finite-horizon POMDPs are PSPACE-complete, while MDPs are P-complete.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Astrom-optimal65}: Founding belief sufficiency; proved only for costs linear in the state law, so our entropy objective needs the MM surrogate to restore it
\end{itemize}
\begin{multicols}{2}
\memb{Other support, 5.} \citetext{\citealp{Smallwood-optimal73}; \citealp{Papadimitriou-complexity87}; \citealp{Singh-learning94}; \citealp{Kaelbling-planning98}; \citealp{Subramanian-approximate20}}

\memb{Other background, 4.} {\footnotesize \citetext{\citealp{Striebel-sufficient65}; \citealp{Astrom-optimal69}\dag; \citealp{Sondik-optimal78}; \citealp{Mundhenk-complexity00}}}

\end{multicols}

\subsection{S5/belief-as-state/belief-dependent-objectives}\label{node:S5-S5-belief-as-state-belief-dependent-objectives}
\textbf{Relation} $\approx$: Same mechanism, opposite sign. These objectives reward a certain belief, or maximise entropy. Ours penalises outcome entropy, whose budget H(Z) = H(Z\textbar{}\ensuremath{\theta}) + I(\ensuremath{\theta};Z) carries +I(\ensuremath{\theta};Z).
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item **\citet{Hijab-entropy84}**, the 1984 ancestor. In adaptive LQ with unknown \ensuremath{\theta}, the value of Feldbaum's cost is S = \ensuremath{\tfrac12} \ensuremath{\Sigma} \ensuremath{\pi}\_k x\textsuperscript{T}K\_k x + H(\ensuremath{\pi}, \ensuremath{\pi}\textsuperscript{0}), with H = \ensuremath{-}\ensuremath{\Sigma} \ensuremath{\pi} log(\ensuremath{\pi}/\ensuremath{\pi}\textsuperscript{0}), Eq 12. The posterior-entropy term emerges from the value function instead of being added.
\item **\citet{Feng-active97}** minimises the posterior state entropy E log \ensuremath{\Delta}\_k of a quantised scalar loop, J = \ensuremath{\Sigma} E c\_k[\ensuremath{\Delta}\_k], Eq 79. It is reduced to a finite controlled Markov chain with DP, and for integer a stationary Markov policies are optimal. Destabilising laws send I(x\_k;Q\_k) to +\ensuremath{\infty}, so probing and regulating conflict.
\item **\citet{Greigarn-taskoriented19}** greedily minimises the posterior entropy of the task-optimal action \ensuremath{\pi}(X), Eqs 11--13. It has our sign on a task-relative belief quantity, but it is myopic, uses a fixed policy class, and has no vertex argument.
\item \textbf{Prior art for our linear-in-b move.} Spaan et al.'s POMDP-IR adds prediction actions so the information reward is linear in b and the value stays PWLC. After the MM step our reward \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_z \ensuremath{\bar{K}}(z\textbar{}b,a) ln q(z) is linear in b by the same device, so our problem is an ordinary POMDP, not a \ensuremath{\rho}-POMDP. Read at abstract level.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Rewards written on the belief, \ensuremath{\rho}(b), convex. Examples: negative belief entropy for active sensing, smoother entropy, state-entropy maximisation in POMDPs with its "hallucination" failure, EFE as a \ensuremath{\rho}-POMDP utility, and the posterior-entropy cost of quantised control.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Araya-pomdp10}: rho-POMDP: convex belief-dependent reward, the closest formal neighbour of our belief-MDP objective and of opposite sign; gap 2 anchor
\end{itemize}

\medskip\textsc{bayes-optimal}

\subsection{S5/bayes-optimal/bamdp}\label{node:S5-S5-bayes-optimal-bamdp}
\textit{Merged from} S5/bayes-optimal/bamdp/pseudo-rewards (11); S5/bayes-optimal/amortised-meta (22). \textbf{Relation} $=$/\textsf{C}/\textsf{T}: Same objective on the reward channel. There, min H[d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} \ensuremath{\bar{K}}] = F(\ensuremath{\hat{R}}(\ensuremath{\pi})) is maximisation of the Bayesian return.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Gittins' DAI theorem is proved by the linear vertex argument, a weighted average never exceeds the max. The index needs frozen arms, so it fails under a moving MM reward.
\item Arumugam--Singh warn that hidden redraws make the information horizon infinite, and that is exactly our persistent-disturbance regime.
\item \citet{Androulakis-generalised14} plays a zero-sum game in which nature maximises a concave generalised entropy over priors. Its Remark 1 says the equalising policy may be stochastic, so the vertex result fails under minimax over models. This scopes our theorem to a fixed prior. [S5/bayes-optimal/bamdp/pseudo-rewards] Contrast. Our belief-MDP objective is not a pseudo-reward: on K\_\ensuremath{\rho} it is the Bayesian return itself. Lidayan classes SMiRL as "no VOI, repulsive VOO". A posterior-variance bonus, as in Sorg and in O'Donoghue's UBE, is optimism, the opposite sign to what an entropy minimiser adds. Kolter--Ng's BEB solves the mean MDP on a frozen belief with bonus \ensuremath{\beta}/(1+\ensuremath{\alpha}\textsubscript{0}), and Thm 2 shows that bonuses decaying faster than n\^{}\{\ensuremath{-}1/2\} are not PAC-MDP. [S5/bayes-optimal/amortised-meta] A tool we use. It is the scalable route for the belief-MDP variant beyond Beta counts, and Mikulik's decoding protocol lets us check the learned state against the posterior. F's Zamboni read adds a caveat: an amortised belief that is free to sharpen can lower believed outcome entropy by overconfidence.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Put the posterior over the unknown MDP into the state. The optimal policy on the hyperstate is the Bayes-optimal learner. The line starts with Bellman--Kalaba 1959 Sec 7, which puts the counts (m, n) of a Beta posterior into the state of a DP functional equation. It runs through Feldbaum's dual control series, Gittins, Duff, BEETLE, BA-POMDP, BAMCP, information horizon and Bayesian exploration networks. \citet{Feldbaum-problems60}, the paper on disk, is not dual control. It analyses a fixed extremum-seeking law u\_\{n+1\} \ensuremath{-} u\_n = \ensuremath{-}a sign \ensuremath{\delta}w\_n as a Markov chain, with search time (h+1)/(1\ensuremath{-}2p[h]), Eq 9. It is the probe-versus-operate precursor.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S5/bayes-optimal/bamdp/pseudo-rewards [C]: Exploration bonuses read as approximations to BAMDP value: VOI, BEB, variance rewards, the uncertainty Bellman equation, knowledge gradient.
\item Absorbed S5/bayes-optimal/amortised-meta [T]: Memory-based meta-learning approximates Bayes-optimal agents, and the recurrent state decodes to the posterior.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Lidayan-bamdp25}: BAMDP value = VOI + VOO; classes SMiRL as repulsive VOO; positions our belief-MDP objective as the BAMDP objective itself, not a pseudo-reward; owned by B5
\end{itemize}

\subsection{S5/bayes-optimal/limits-of-bayes}\label{node:S5-S5-bayes-optimal-limits-of-bayes}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition or limitation. It scopes our theorem to Bayes-optimality given the prior.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Hutter 2005 Sec 1.4.1: deterministic value-optimal policies suffice, and V*\_\ensuremath{\rho} is convex in the environment mixture, Thm 5.21.
\item Lattimore--Hutter 2011: no computable weak asymptotically optimal policy exists, Thm 8, and the incomputable one of Thm 11 uses random exploration bursts. So determinism does not survive asymptotic optimality in learning.
\item Leike 2015's dogmatic prior makes any computable policy uniquely Bayes-optimal, Thm 7: a formal dark room for an entropy minimiser.
\item Shannon-KSA is the literal opposite of our objective, and its noise fix mirrors our split H(Z\textbar{}\ensuremath{\theta}) + I(\ensuremath{\theta};Z). Recoverability is the dark-room and absorbing-death issue.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Bayes-optimal agents over universal mixtures and their limits. Bayes-optimality depends on the prior. Bayes-optimal agents are generally not asymptotically optimal, while Thompson sampling is, Leike 2016, Thm 4. Shannon-KSA maximises predictive observation entropy and is fooled by noise. KL-KSA subtracts H(O\textbar{}\ensuremath{\theta}).

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Leike-thompson16}: Bayes-optimal agents are not asymptotically optimal while TS is, Thm 4; our deterministic belief regulator is Bayes-optimal only
\end{itemize}

\medskip\textsc{approx-exploration}

\subsection{S5/approx-exploration/posterior-sampling}\label{node:S5-S5-approx-exploration-posterior-sampling}
\textit{Merged from} S5/approx-exploration/information-directed (10). \textbf{Relation} \textsf{T}/\textsf{L}: A tool and a baseline, with a variational justification. Ortega--Braun's Bayesian control rule derives Thompson-style acting as the minimiser of a relative entropy in which actions are interventions. Read at abstract level. Our Thompson variant samples only the channel. Each sampled subproblem is concave in d\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} and so commits to a deterministic policy, which is the property deep exploration needs. By Tarbouriech's Lemma 8 the mean occupancy of TS is P(\ensuremath{\Gamma}\emph{), a mixture of those vertices. It is not Bayes-optimal. [S5/approx-exploration/information-directed] A limitation on scope, plus a tool. In the "known standard" example every deterministic stationary belief policy has linear regret for some prior. Our vertex result is therefore a Bayes-average claim, not a regret claim. Russo--Van Roy bound E Regret \ensuremath{\leq} \ensuremath{\surd}(\ensuremath{\Gamma} H(A}) T), Prop 1. For our Thompson variant the relevant entropy is over optimal vertices. Value-equivalent sampling formalises "carry only the value-relevant posterior".

\textbf{Idea.} Act optimally for one posterior sample: Thompson, PSRL, RLSVI, bootstrapped DQN, randomised priors, epinets and joint predictive distributions, and double Thompson sampling for RLHF queries. It includes regret analysis of TS and the DeepSea and bsuite benchmarks, where dithering needs 2\^{}N episodes. Thompson 1933 allocates with probability P = Pr(p\textsubscript{2} \textgreater{} p\textsubscript{1} \textbar{} data) under Beta posteriors: two-arm probability matching, which is one posterior draw.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S5/approx-exploration/information-directed [L]: Minimise the information ratio \ensuremath{\Delta}\textsuperscript{2}/g: IDS, the information-theoretic analysis of TS, learning targets smaller than the model, value-equivalent sampling, rate-distortion targets.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Osband-more13}: PSRL: our Thompson variant samples only the channel; the tractable baseline our Bayes-optimal regulator should beat
\item \citet{Osband-deep17}: Origin of DeepSea and deep exploration; pure-exploitation failure is the reward-side analogue of our alphabet-threshold result
\item \citet{Russo-learning14}: IDS known-standard example: every deterministic stationary belief policy has linear regret for some prior; scopes our determinism claim to Bayes-average
\end{itemize}

\subsection{S5/approx-exploration/information-gain}\label{node:S5-S5-approx-exploration-information-gain}
\textbf{Relation} $-$: Opposite sign. These methods reward I(\ensuremath{\theta};Z), and our predictive entropy contains it. Sun et al. is the cleanest contrast pair: the same belief MDP, the same kernels, and the opposite sign on the information term. Two refinements follow.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \textbf{Depeweg et al. have two halves.} The active-learning half maximises I(y;W), Eq 3, the opposite sign. The policy-search half, Eq 11, minimises J plus \ensuremath{\beta}\textsuperscript{2} times the epistemic cost variance and \ensuremath{\gamma}\textsuperscript{2} times the aleatoric cost variance. That is the same sign on another quantity, spread measured by variance, not entropy.
\item \textbf{The noisy-TV trap is the mirror of our noise basin.} Prediction-error curiosity is drawn to irreducible noise, which maximises H(Z\textbar{}S,A). Our minimiser would avoid it. Jarrett et al. fix it by conditioning on hindsight noise, and Mavor-Parker et al. subtract the aleatoric part, r\_i = \ensuremath{\|}s\ensuremath{'} \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\mu}\ensuremath{\|}\textsuperscript{2} \ensuremath{-} \ensuremath{\eta} Tr \ensuremath{\Sigma}. Schmidhuber 1991 already rewards model improvement to avoid unpredictable inputs.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Maximise expected information about the parameters: Lindley, curiosity, optimal Bayesian exploration by DP over histories, VIME, MAX, Plan2Explore, BOED, prediction-error curiosity from learned dynamics models, and goal commands planned for exploration. The aleatoric/epistemic split is H(Y) = E H(Y\textbar{}\ensuremath{\theta}) + I(Y;\ensuremath{\theta}).

\medskip\textsc{persistent-disturbance}

\subsection{S5/persistent-disturbance/nonstationary}\label{node:S5-S5-persistent-disturbance-nonstationary}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition and tool. The notes need a persistent disturbance for the objective to be non-degenerate, and model it as a redraw of \ensuremath{\theta} at rate \ensuremath{\eta}. That is a hidden-Markov restless bandit.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Meshram proves threshold policies. The notes' two-arm check is this known object.
\item Min--Russo bound the per-period regret by \ensuremath{\surd}(\ensuremath{\Gamma}\ensuremath{\cdot}\ensuremath{\bar{H}}(A*)), which answers the notes' \ensuremath{\eta}-versus-ln 2 conjecture.
\item Adams--MacKay's constant hazard H = \ensuremath{\eta} is exactly the redraw model, so BOCPD is the exact posterior feeding the \ensuremath{\hat{q}} refit.
\item Predictive sampling is the tractable baseline.
\item \textbf{One rate is not enough.} Piray--Daw show that volatility raises and observation noise lowers optimal exploration in restless Gaussian bandits. The disturbance model needs both parameters, not \ensuremath{\eta} alone. Read at abstract level.
\item \textbf{Freeze before solving.} Zhang et al.'s mode-mixture Bellman operator is a \ensuremath{\gamma}-contraction with a frozen belief, but its modulus becomes \ensuremath{\gamma} + \ensuremath{\lambda}\ensuremath{\Delta} once the belief depends on Q. That is a concrete parallel for why the MM loop must freeze \ensuremath{\hat{q}}. Read at abstract level.
\item Fedus et al.: a hazard prior gives the discount 1/(1+kt) = \ensuremath{\int}\ensuremath{\gamma}\^{}\{kt\}d\ensuremath{\gamma}, Eq 13. The mixed-\ensuremath{\gamma} occupancy is time-inconsistent, so the vertex argument must be rechecked if the horizon is uncertain.
\end{itemize}


\textbf{Idea.} Hidden, drifting parameters. The node covers restless and hidden-Markov bandits with threshold or Whittle structure, changepoint detection, predictive sampling, entropy-rate regret, continual RL, hyperbolic discounting as hazard uncertainty, and human volatility tracking.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Meshram-whittle16}: Hidden-Markov restless bandit with paid no-sample arm has threshold policies: the notes two-arm restless check is this known object
\end{itemize}

\medskip\textsc{A. Sufficiency: which quotient is enough, 115 records,}

\subsection{A1. reward-abstraction-ladder}\label{node:S6-S6-A-reward-abstraction-ladder}
\textit{Merged from} S6/A/objective-closedness (4). \textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}: Precondition or limitation. Every rung below model-irrelevance is defined by a reward expectation, and the MM reward E\_K ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}} changes at each refit, so only the top rung stays sufficient across the whole MM loop. The top rung is reward-free at its source: Larsen--Skou bisimulation is equality of \ensuremath{\mu}\_\{p,a\} on every class, and Givan's stochastic bisimulation is built over an arbitrary output set O, so taking K(\ensuremath{\cdot}\textbar{}s,a) as the output gives outcome-bisimulation with no new definition. [S6/A/objective-closedness] Precondition or limitation. Outcome entropy is mixture-concave, so it sits outside DP closedness and no value-level abstraction suffices.

\textbf{Idea.} A partial order of state abstractions, model-irrelevance \ensuremath{\succeq} Q\^{}\ensuremath{\pi} \ensuremath{\succeq} Q* \ensuremath{\succeq} a* \ensuremath{\succeq} \ensuremath{\pi}\emph{, with bisimulation metrics as the metric relaxation of the top rung and value equivalence as the decision-aware bottom. Non-Markov rungs are included: extreme aggregation keeps Q} and \ensuremath{\pi}* even when the reduced process is not an MDP \citep{Hutter-extreme14,Majeed-performance18}.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S6/A/objective-closedness [L]: Only exponential utilities and the mean are Bellman-optimisable functionals of the return, and stock-augmented distributional DP is exact iff the objective is indifferent to mixtures and to \ensuremath{\gamma}.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Li-towards06}: Founding ladder of abstractions; every rung below model-irrelevance is a reward expectation, so our objective must sit on the top rung relative to Z
\item \citet{AvilaPires-optimizing25}: Published criterion placing mixture-concave outcome entropy outside distributional DP closedness
\end{itemize}

\subsection{A2. outcome-relative-equivalence, the central gap node}\label{node:S6-S6-A-outcome-relative-equivalence}
\textit{Merged from} S6/A/action-sufficiency-notions (4). \textbf{Relation} $\supset$/$\subset$: Generalisation of what we need, stated for reward or labels. With Z in place of reward, Castro's hierarchy orders the note's notions: one-step consequence equivalence is the coarsest, then trace equivalence, then outcome-bisimulation \ensuremath{\Gamma}*, which a closed-loop regulator needs. Replacing z by ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}(z) in Veness's estimator gives \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_z ln \ensuremath{\hat{q}}(z) \ensuremath{\nu}(z\textbar{}s,a), which is the MM step's Q-function, for a finite outcome alphabet. [S6/A/action-sufficiency-notions] The note's Definition is the consequence-side notion our objective needs; the other two are special cases or contrasts. The exact statements are in digest.md.

\textbf{Idea.} Equivalences defined by the distribution of an outcome: bisimulation with reward distributions, trace equivalence, Z\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}-irrelevance, reward-predictive clustering, labelled or emission bisimulation, property-relative abstraction, telic states. The node also holds one estimator of the per-pair outcome law: Compress and Control writes Q\^{}\ensuremath{\pi}(s,a) = \ensuremath{\Sigma}\_z z \ensuremath{\nu}(z\textbar{}s,a) and gets \ensuremath{\nu} by Bayes from density models \ensuremath{\rho}\_S(s\textbar{}z,a) and \ensuremath{\rho}\_Z(z\textbar{}a).
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S6/A/action-sufficiency-notions [\ensuremath{\subset}]: Three unrelated definitions under one name, plus task- and control-sufficient variants.
\end{itemize}


\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Castro-equivalence09}: Closest precedent: bisimulation with reward distributions and the trace hierarchy; read with Z for reward it gives outcome-bisimulation
\item \citet{Liu-returnbased21}: Only learned distribution-relative abstraction, Z\^{}pi-irrelevance, with a contrastive loss that resists collapse; our learning recipe
\item \citet{Huang-action22}: Meaning 1 of action-sufficient, graphical and reward-relative; also the keep-ancestors-of-the-target rule we would re-target to Z
\item \citet{Hyeon-actionsufficient26}: Meaning 2 of action-sufficient, I(A;G\textbar{}S,Z)=0; value level sets merge codes needing opposite actions, which applies to a scalar ln q reward
\end{itemize}

\subsection{A3. information-states}\label{node:S6-S6-A-information-states}
\textbf{Relation} $\supset$: Generalisation of consequence equivalence to histories and all horizons. It also holds the opposite-sign sibling: maximising predictive information gives periodic loops, Marzen, minimising H(Z) gives fixed points. \citet{Dai-worth26} scores which model elements to keep by the planning capacity they give, a sufficiency criterion that is neither reward nor prediction.

\textbf{Idea.} Minimal history statistics for predicting action-conditioned futures: causal states, \ensuremath{\varepsilon}-transducers, PSRs, AIS, agent states, utile suffix memory, Still's interactive partition. Rosas et al. prove the \ensuremath{\varepsilon}-transducer is the unique minimal predictive model of an interface p(y\_\{:t\} \textbar{} a\_\{:\}), and that bisimulation reduces transducers.

\subsection{A6. equivariance-homomorphisms}\label{node:S6-S6-A-equivariance-homomorphisms}
\textbf{Relation} \textsf{T}: Tool we use, via the note's equivariance section. Group-structured outcome coordinates would carry an invariant Haar measure, a natural \ensuremath{\mu}.

\textbf{Idea.} Latent transitions equivariant under actions, f(a\ensuremath{\cdot}o) = g\_a(f(o)); sensorimotor contingency theory is the classical root. A soft version regularises action effects to be state-invariant \citep{Saanum-simplifying24}.

\subsection{B1. forward-self-prediction}\label{node:S6-S6-B-forward-self-prediction}
\textit{Merged from} S6 (surveys) (3). \textbf{Relation} \textsf{L}/\textsf{T}: Precondition or limitation. The note's \texttt{eq:fwd-loss} is DeepMDP's L\_P without L\_R and has the constant minimiser. An entropy gradient into this encoder breaks the conservation law that prevents collapse, so the regulator's objective must not train the encoder. Taking the auxiliary target to be Z turns the Yu25 guarantee into a separation of outcome-bisimulation classes, which is an objective-level anti-collapse route for the note's forward loss. [S6, surveys,] Tool we use: overviews for positioning.

\textbf{Idea.} Learn z by predicting its own future, DeepMDP, SPR, BYOL-type, JEPA, RSSM, EfficientZero, TACO, by maximising forward MI, or by factorising the forward kernel as a low-rank MDP (FLAMBE, Uehara21). Non-collapse usually comes from the training dynamics: stop-gradient, EMA, a fast predictor. It can also come from the objective: \citet{Yu-auxiliary25} proves that in a deterministic MDP zero latent-consistency loss plus zero auxiliary-regression loss separates any two states whose auxiliary-target futures differ.
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item Absorbed S6, surveys, [T]: Surveys of state representation learning for RL, kept outside the A to E tree.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{B2. inverse-dynamics-endogenous-state}\label{node:S6-S6-B-inverse-dynamics-endogenous-state}
\textbf{Relation} $\approx$: Same mechanism, different objective. The note's \texttt{prop:anti-collapse} floor is E\ensuremath{\|}A \ensuremath{-} EA\ensuremath{\|}\textsuperscript{2}, which vanishes under a deterministic data policy, and our optimum is deterministic. AC-State's range minimisation is H(Z) \ensuremath{\leq} log\textbar{}Range\textbar{} minimised subject to inverse optimality.

\textbf{Idea.} Predict a\_t from (z\_t, z\_\{t+1\}), one-step or multi-step, with a minimum-cardinality bottleneck that provably recovers the endogenous state in Ex-BMDPs. From video, latent actions absorb exogenous motion.

\memb{Core.}
\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=1.2em]
\item \citet{Lamb-guaranteed23}: AC-State: range minimisation subject to multistep inverse optimality, the nearest object to minimising latent entropy against counting measure
\item \citet{Ivashkov-smwm26}: Already contains the inverse-dynamics anti-collapse argument of the action-sufficient note, Prop. anti-collapse; must be cited as prior
\end{itemize}
